- Root cause analysis: I invoked an optional formatter without first verifying that the repository config or environment provided it.
- How fixed: The failed command was read-only. I retained the passing test and `git diff --check` validation paths and did not install a new dependency.

## 2026-09-14: Used obsolete Graphify update syntax

- Issue: I invoked `graphify <path> --update`, which this installed CLI parsed as a full semantic extraction and then failed because its configured Bedrock model is retired.
- Root cause analysis: I followed the installed skill's older usage form without checking the current CLI command syntax first.
- How fixed: I checked `graphify --help` and reran the supported code-only incremental command, `graphify update <path>`, which refreshed the main graph successfully without an LLM.

## 2026-09-16: Markdown backticks broke in-app help TypeScript

- Issue: I inserted Markdown backticks around `TERMINAI_WHISPER_MODEL` and `ggml-base.en.bin` inside the `USER_GUIDE_MD` template literal, causing the HelpModal test transform to fail before collection.
- Root cause analysis: I copied Markdown-safe guide wording into a JavaScript template literal without escaping embedded backticks or checking the file's string context.
- How fixed: I removed the inline-code backticks from the in-app help copy, reran the focused AgentPanel and HelpModal tests, and kept the Markdown backticks only in the external `.md` guide.

# Mistake: scoped the editor toolbar design to IaC Studio only

- What the issue/mistake was: I treated the requested code-editor toolbar as an IaC Studio-only change, even though the standalone code editor window uses the same Monaco editor surface and also needs the toolbar.
- RCA of the Issue: I followed the first graph/code hit into `IacStudioTab` and did not immediately trace the parallel `EditorTab` path before presenting the first design.
- How you fixed it: I re-queried Graphify and read `EditorTab`, `EditorPane`, and detached editor flow before revising the design to cover both editor surfaces with shared toolbar behavior.

# Mistake: patched IaC test using a helper from another file

- What the issue/mistake was: I attempted to edit `IacStudioTab.test.tsx` as if it already had the `mocks.monacoProps` helper from `EditorPane.test.tsx`, so the exact edit failed.
- RCA of the Issue: I carried over a nearby test pattern from memory instead of using the just-read IaC test shape.
- How you fixed it: The failed edit made no file changes. I patched the actual IaC test structure by adding a local hoisted editor stub and updating the existing MonacoEditor mock.

# Mistake: used fd even though the shell did not provide it

- What the issue/mistake was: I used `fd` to search for sidecar lock files, but the active shell environment does not have `fd` installed.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed a developer convenience tool was available instead of using the repository's already-available ripgrep inventory command.
- How you fixed it: The failed command was read-only. I reran the discovery with `rg --files` scoped to sidecar dependency files and found `sidecar/uv.lock`.

# Mistake: assumed `python` was on PATH in zsh

- What the issue/mistake was: I ran a small cleanup script with `python`, but this shell has no `python` executable on PATH.
- RCA of the Issue: I ignored the repository's existing `uv run --project sidecar python ...` pattern and assumed a generic interpreter alias existed.
- How you fixed it: The failed command made no changes. I kept the working code as-is and will use the managed sidecar interpreter or direct Edit calls for follow-up Python snippets.

# Mistake: applied a Kanban patch against outdated local context

- What the issue/mistake was: Several Kanban patch applications failed because their expected transition, test, regex, and catalog snippets did not match the current file.
- RCA of the Issue: I grouped edits using remembered formatting instead of reading the current rustfmt output before each patch.
- How you fixed it: Failed patches made no code changes. I re-read the target sections and applied the required edits in smaller patches; I left the last optional nested-catalog adjustment unapplied.

# Mistake: formatted unrelated AI command code

- What the issue/mistake was: Running rustfmt on `commands/ai.rs` changed unrelated formatting in the existing large module.
- RCA of the Issue: I applied file-wide formatting instead of limiting formatting checks to the new Kanban module.
- How you fixed it: I restored the unrelated hunks and kept only the required visibility change for the topolograph binding.
# Mistake: first nested catalog namespacing draft mutated a temporary copy

- What the issue/mistake was: The first collision fix passed a cloned catalog object to the namespacing helper, which would have left the real catalog unchanged.
- RCA of the Issue: I moved the helper call into place before reconciling its ownership with the existing mutable map borrow.
- How you fixed it: I changed the helper to mutate the catalog map directly and added an assertion for both outer and nested function names before running the focused Rust test.

# Mistake: test app build attempted updater signing

- What the issue/mistake was: The first packaged Kanban test build reached updater artifact signing and failed because the environment had no configured private signing key.
- RCA of the Issue: I did not check the Tauri bundle configuration before packaging; `createUpdaterArtifacts` is enabled for release builds, but a local UI test app does not need updater artifacts.
- How you fixed it: Disable updater artifact creation for the test-only build and set the test app identifier at compile time as well as bundle time.

# Mistake: Kanban redesign test used stale accessibility assumptions

- What the issue/mistake was: The first UI test runs could not find board lanes and task fields using the old accessible names after the redesign.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed a labelled `<section>` would expose a region role and kept lowercase/status and task-detail selectors from the old form.
- How you fixed it: I added explicit lane regions and button labels, updated the test to use the visible capitalized lane and form labels, and reran the focused UI test successfully.

# Mistake: described Kanban context as a live terminal attachment

- What the issue/mistake was: I told the user to attach a terminal pane for the Kanban smoke test, but the UI only accepts pasted context and does not offer a live pane selector.
- RCA of the Issue: I inferred live attachment from the plan wording without checking the actual form or tracing the terminal context through the agent request. The Rust code sent a string as a capability object, so Sidecar ignored it.
- How you fixed it: I corrected the instructions, changed Kanban to include pasted terminal text in the task prompt as reference-only context, and removed the invalid capability parameter.

# Mistake: Kanban stylesheet used literal colors

- What the issue/mistake was: The repository's structural-color check rejected hard-coded colors in the new Kanban stylesheet.
- RCA of the Issue: I used literal lane and modal colors without checking the repository's semantic token requirement.
- How you fixed it: Replaced the literals with the existing status, inverse-text, and backdrop tokens, then reran the check.

# Mistake: chose the wrong foreground token for the Kanban action button

- What the issue/mistake was: The Kanban action button used `text-inverse`, which failed the theme contrast check against the accent background.
- RCA of the Issue: I selected a general inverse token instead of the repository's purpose-built `text-on-strong-accent` token.
- How you fixed it: Switched the button to `text-on-strong-accent` and reran the theme contrast check.

# Mistake: diagnostic read emitted a local agent prompt line

- What the issue/mistake was: A file inspection command printed a line from a local agent prompt that contained an environment identifier.
- RCA of the Issue: I used a line-range read across the prompt body instead of extracting only the needed frontmatter metadata.
- How you fixed it: Stopped reading prompt bodies, excluded the identifier from further output, and switched to parsing only agent IDs, catalog references, and tool names.

# Mistake: runtime interpreter probe used an unquoted workspace path

- What the issue/mistake was: A diagnostic command failed because the shell split the workspace path at its space.
- RCA of the Issue: I passed an absolute path without quoting or escaping it.
- How you fixed it: Reran the probe using a path relative to the repository root.

# Mistake: portable smoke-test path was assumed to be repository-rooted

- What the issue/mistake was: I referenced the portable-sidecar smoke script from the repository root, but it lives under sidecar/scripts/.
- RCA of the Issue: I searched for the invocation but did not follow the path used by build_sidecar.sh.
- How you fixed it: Located the script at its invoked path and continued from there.

# Mistake: first PyATS fix patch used stale surrounding context

- What the issue/mistake was: The first multi-file patch did not apply because its context did not match the current Rust comment and sidecar build script.
- RCA of the Issue: I grouped edits before checking the exact current insertion point in each file.
- How you fixed it: I inspected the live file context and applied the changes as separate, narrow patches.

# Mistake: release patch used stale sidecar build-script context

- What the issue/mistake was: The first attempt to port the PyATS fix to the clean release checkout used an insertion context from the older checkout, so the patch did not apply.
- RCA of the Issue: I checked that the target function names existed but did not inspect the exact adjacent build-script lines before applying the combined patch.
- How you fixed it: I stopped after the patch was rejected, inspected the current release-branch context, and will apply the change using the exact live lines.

# Mistake: concurrent broad suites shared the sidecar runtime

- What the issue/mistake was: The full Python and Rust test suites were started concurrently; both exercise the sidecar runtime and stalled instead of producing complete results.
- RCA of the Issue: I parallelized suites that launch sidecar processes and may share runtime resources, so their results were not isolated.
- How you fixed it: I stopped both broad runs, verified the Rust sidecar heartbeat test in isolation, and will rerun the suites serially.

# Mistake: sanitizer report introduced trailing Markdown spaces

- What the issue/mistake was: The refreshed release sanitizer report used trailing spaces for Markdown hard breaks, so `git diff --check` rejected it.
- RCA of the Issue: I formatted the report with an unnecessary hard-break convention and did not run the whitespace check before presenting the diff.
- How you fixed it: I removed the trailing spaces and reran `git diff --check`.

# Mistake: direct heartbeat test streamed a configured device label

- What the issue/mistake was: The direct heartbeat test's execution logger emitted one configured device label in tool output.
- RCA of the Issue: I ran the executor without capturing its verbose execution log stream, then noticed that the logger includes snippets from code.
- How you fixed it: I limited follow-up inspection to parsed status and PyATS verb names and did not retain or repeat the label.

# Mistake: compatibility build started with an isolated empty Cargo target

- What the issue/mistake was: The first compatibility-build command omitted the existing Cargo target directory, so Cargo began recompiling dependencies in the temporary worktree.
- RCA of the Issue: I did not pass the known local target directory explicitly when building from a detached worktree.
- How you fixed it: I stopped the build before it produced an app and will rerun it with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` set to the existing release checkout's target directory.

# Mistake: Actions status parser command had invalid shell quoting

- What the issue/mistake was: Read-only Python commands intended to parse the public GitHub Actions page first failed because embedded single quotes ended the shell string, then returned no matches because the regex backslash was over-escaped.
- RCA of the Issue: I did not validate the shell quoting and regex string as passed through the command tool.
- How you fixed it: I am simplifying the pattern to avoid backslashes and conflicting quotes, then rerunning the status check; no repository or GitHub state was changed by these failed checks.

# Mistake: repeated invalid Bun cwd flag placement

- What the issue/mistake was: I retried the frontend build with the same invalid `bun --cwd ... run build` argument ordering after Bun had already printed the correct `bun run --cwd ... build` shape.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed Bun's global `--cwd` worked before `run` and did not adapt to the CLI help text after the first failure.
- How you fixed it: I reran the frontend validation as `bun run --cwd '/Users/shaunwhi/CCIE Terminal' build:relaxed`; the relaxed project build passed. Full `build` remains blocked by unrelated repo-wide TypeScript/test typing debt.

# Mistake: build log wrapper used zsh's read-only status variable

- What the issue/mistake was: The build log wrapper completed Cargo and then failed when assigning to `status`, so it did not report Cargo's actual result.
- RCA of the Issue: `status` is a read-only special variable in zsh.
- How you fixed it: Reran the command with a `build_rc` variable and captured the build output before inspecting it.

# Mistake: isolated release worktree lacked the packaged Whisper model

- What the issue/mistake was: Cargo's Tauri build script stopped because `resources/models/ggml-base.en.bin` was absent in the detached worktree.
- RCA of the Issue: The source checkout has only a placeholder at that path, while the actual model is fetched during Tauri's frontend build step.
- How you fixed it: Copied the model from the already-installed release bundle into the temporary worktree only; the source checkout and user database were unchanged.

# Mistake: Tauri resource directory was omitted from the detached worktree

- What the issue/mistake was: The next Cargo build stopped because the configured `../sidecar/dist/python` resource directory was absent.
- RCA of the Issue: The release build depends on a generated, ignored directory that is not present in a fresh Git worktree.
- How you fixed it: Created the empty directory in the temporary worktree; the compatibility app will reuse the packaged Python runtime from the installed release bundle.

# Mistake: compatibility executable was built without the frontend assets

- What the issue/mistake was: The compatibility process and sidecar started, but macOS reported no app window.
- RCA of the Issue: I bypassed the Tauri release CLI with a direct Cargo build. That initial build lacked `frontendDist`; adding the assets alone still left the window absent because the production Tauri build configuration had not been applied.
- How you fixed it: Ran the project Tauri release build with the exact release frontend and bundled Python runtime, copied its app bundle to the separate compatibility path, and ad-hoc signed and verified that local bundle. macOS then reported the app frontmost with one window.

# Mistake: read-only migration query used the wrong history table

- What the issue/mistake was: My first read-only SQLite query looked for `_sqlx_migrations`, but this app records migrations in `refinery_schema_history`.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed the migration framework's table name instead of checking the live database schema first.
- How you fixed it: Listed the database tables and queried `refinery_schema_history`; it confirms migrations 92 (`kanban`), 91, and 90. The failed query made no database changes.

# Mistake: compared commits using a cross-repository path as a revision

- What the issue/mistake was: A read-only `git diff` treated the Network-TerminAI filesystem path as a revision prefix and failed with `bad revision`.
- RCA of the Issue: I used Git's revision syntax for a second repository instead of running the comparison from that repository or using `git -C`.
- How you fixed it: Inspected each repository's commit and parent separately and will transfer only the Kanban feature patch into a clean branch; no Git history or worktree state was changed by the failed comparison.

# Mistake: assumed the Kanban patch would apply to public main without context changes

- What the issue/mistake was: The patch check found that the Tauri startup hunk in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` no longer matches public main.
- RCA of the Issue: The Kanban commit was authored on a separate checkout and the public startup code has moved since that base.
- How you fixed it: I stopped before applying the patch, isolated the only reported conflict, and am adapting that registration to the current Tauri startup block while keeping the other feature files unchanged.

# Mistake: ran the Rust format check from the repository root

- What the issue/mistake was: `cargo fmt -- --check` ran from the repository root, which has no Cargo manifest, so the combined verification command stopped before preflight and diff checks.
- RCA of the Issue: The Rust crate manifest is under `src-tauri/`, but I ran formatting with the frontend checks from the repository root.
- How you fixed it: I am rerunning `cargo fmt -- --check` from `src-tauri/` and the release preflight and diff checks from the repository root as separate commands; nothing was modified by the failed check.

# Mistake: shadowed the exec tool namespace in a read-only check

- What the issue/mistake was: A small JavaScript inspection wrapper declared a local variable named `tools`, shadowing the injected tool namespace and failing before any commands ran.
- RCA of the Issue: I reused the namespace name for a Promise result variable.
- How you fixed it: I will use a distinct result variable name and rerun the read-only lookups; no command was launched and no repository state changed.

# Mistake: imported Kanban runner explicitly dropped a Tauri state handle

- What the issue/mistake was: Clippy rejected `drop(state)` because `tauri::State` does not implement `Drop`.
- RCA of the Issue: The imported code used explicit drop to end the borrowed state before an async call instead of limiting the handle's scope.
- How you fixed it: Cloned the needed values inside a lexical block so the handle's borrow ends naturally before awaiting; the focused and full Rust suites had passed before this lint-only correction and will be rerun.

# Mistake: invoked pytest for stdlib unittest release checks

- What the issue/mistake was: The release test command used `python3 -m pytest`, but the system Python has no pytest module and the release tests use `unittest`.
- RCA of the Issue: I did not inspect the release test files before choosing the runner.
- How you fixed it: I am switching to `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests`; the failed pytest command made no source changes.

# Mistake: sanitizer report used trailing-space Markdown hard breaks

- What the issue/mistake was: `git diff --check` found trailing spaces on three sanitizer report metadata lines.
- RCA of the Issue: I used Markdown hard-break whitespace for metadata formatting.
- How you fixed it: Replaced those lines with a plain list without trailing spaces; no source content was affected.

# Mistake: passed two Cargo test filters as positional arguments

- What the issue/mistake was: I invoked `cargo test` with two test-name filters, so Cargo rejected the second filter as an unexpected argument.
- RCA of the Issue: I treated Cargo's test filter like a list of patterns instead of its single-filter CLI contract.
- How you fixed it: Reran the two focused Rust tests as separate `cargo test` commands; both passed and the failed command changed no source state.

# Mistake: repeated ignored Plans path in a batch stage

- What the issue/mistake was: I batched `Plans/Mistakes.MD` into `git add` again, and Git stopped on the ignored `Plans` path before staging the intended release files.
- RCA of the Issue: I failed to apply the existing repository lesson that tracked ignored files need a separate update-style stage command.
- How you fixed it: I recorded this recurrence and will stage normal paths separately from `git add -u -- Plans/Mistakes.MD`.

# Mistake: release push targeted the wrong remote repository

- What the issue/mistake was: I pushed the Windows fixes and release bump to `sw4481/TerminAI` even though Shaun expected the CI run in `sw4481/Network-TerminAI`.
- RCA of the Issue: I treated the checkout's `origin` remote as authoritative without reconciling it against Shaun's GitHub Actions URL before pushing.
- How you fixed it: I stopped using the ambiguous checkout remote for the corrective release, fetched `Network-TerminAI/main` into an isolated temp worktree, ported only the intended patches, and will push explicitly to `git@github.com:sw4481/Network-TerminAI.git`.

# Mistake: generated a broad patch against the wrong baseline

- What the issue/mistake was: My first attempt to port the prompt saver diff compared the old branch against the newer Network-TerminAI baseline, producing a patch that tried to apply unrelated historical changes.
- RCA of the Issue: I used a branch-to-branch diff instead of the single commit-local range for the prompt saver change.
- How you fixed it: I reset the temp worktree back to `network-terminai/main`, cleaned the failed application, and reapplied only `3d922a8^..3d922a8` and `d876c06^..d876c06` with explicit exclusions.

# Mistake: conflict resolution inserted shell-script garbage text

- What the issue/mistake was: While resolving `sidecar/scripts/build_sidecar.sh`, I accidentally wrote malformed text after the prune-stage echo line.
- RCA of the Issue: I edited the conflict hunk manually and did not immediately re-read the exact edited lines before moving on.
- How you fixed it: I re-read the file, restored the line to `echo ">> pruning to shrink the bundle"`, and verified no conflict markers remained.

# Mistake: temp sidecar pytest used the wrong dependency mode

- What the issue/mistake was: The first temp-worktree sidecar pytest run failed because `pytest` was not installed in the default uv environment, and an earlier direct `uv run pytest` used a Python context that could not import `deepagents`.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed the temp checkout's default uv run environment included dev dependencies and a stable pytest entrypoint.
- How you fixed it: I reran the regression with `uv run --project sidecar --extra dev python -m pytest`, which installed the existing dev extra and passed the focused subagent tests.

# Mistake: combined multiple revisions with git rev-parse --short

- What the issue/mistake was: The remote fast-forward preflight command failed after fetching because I ran `git rev-parse --short FETCH_HEAD HEAD`, which expects a single revision with this option.
- RCA of the Issue: I compressed display and ancestry checks into one command and forgot that `--short` is not a multi-revision output mode here.
- How you fixed it: I split the commit-display checks into separate `rev-parse --short` calls and kept `merge-base --is-ancestor` as its own preflight.

# Mistake: overwrote existing MonacoEditor CSS while adding cursor fallback

- What the issue/mistake was: I created `MonacoEditor.css` with only the cursor rule and temporarily removed existing Zed overlay and bookmark glyph styles in the temp release worktree.
- RCA of the Issue: I treated the file as new because it was not part of the previous patch summary, instead of checking whether it already existed at HEAD before writing it.
- How you fixed it: I restored the existing CSS from `HEAD` and appended only the minimal cursor visibility rule.

## 2026-10-05 - Pushed follow-up before rerunning changed Rust formatting gate

**Issue/Mistake:** I pushed commit `3bcca8c` after editing `src-tauri/src/pty.rs` without rerunning `scripts/check-rustfmt-changed.sh` after the final Rust change. The GitHub `release-contract` job failed at the changed-file rustfmt step.

**RCA:** I treated earlier local verification as still valid after making a subsequent Rust edit. The project has a narrow changed-Rust formatting gate that must be rerun after every Rust modification, even if `cargo check` passes.

**How I fixed it:** Ran `rustfmt --edition 2021 --config skip_children=true` on `src-tauri/src/pty.rs`, reran `scripts/check-rustfmt-changed.sh`, and kept that check in the final verification list before repushing.

# Mistake: guessed Windows clippy root cause without log evidence

- What the issue/mistake was: I pushed `d400abe` assuming Windows clippy was failing on the PowerShell `to_ascii_lowercase()` helper, but CI still failed in the Windows clippy job.
- RCA of the Issue: GitHub raw logs were blocked, and I treated a plausible Windows-only warning as sufficient evidence instead of auditing all Rust changes visible to the Windows clippy job first.
- How you fixed it: I inspected the full Rust diff from `v1.1.6` to HEAD, found the additional Windows-only LSP import/creation-flags surface, removed the unnecessary trait import, and reran rustfmt, native clippy, cargo check, and diff checks before the next push.

# Mistake: guessed discovery paths and an unavailable skill during Windows triage

- What the issue/mistake was: Read-only triage probes referenced absent `src/lib/platform.ts`, root `tests`, `src/test`, `src/state/tabs.ts`, and guessed PTY, agent, RPC, Meraki, and packaging filenames. A subagent also attempted a Superpowers skill name unavailable in the active skill registry.
- RCA of the Issue: We inferred conventional file locations and invocation availability instead of using source inventory, symbol search, and the exposed skill list first.
- How you fixed it: Located actual platform patterns and `pty_write` in `commands/mod.rs` through scoped searches; used `src-tauri/tests` and the installed Superpowers skill files. Failed probes changed no source or runtime state.
- Additional discovery error: A probe assumed `gh` availability and left a URL wildcard unquoted. Checked available tooling and retried the read-only fetch with isolated Python standard-library HTTP, without executing downloaded content.
- SSH discovery recurrence: Probes guessed absent `terminal_agent/ssh.rs`, a sidecar terminal-agent directory, `platform.rs`, and pane-context paths. Architecture assumptions caused nonexistent-path errors; switched to `rg --files` and the actual `pane_context/{manager,types}.rs` and terminal-agent modules. Failed probes changed no files.
- Release inventory recurrence: A broad version-file inventory included generated `src-tauri/target` artifacts and produced noisy output. The search omitted build-directory exclusions; narrowed subsequent discovery to canonical manifests/scripts and did not read those generated artifacts.
- Follow-up discovery recurrence: Probed an absent Superpowers `commands` directory and attempted `superpowers:systematic-debugging` after finding its cached files, but the active Skill registry reported unknown skill. RCA: confused an installed cache with exposed invocation availability. Recovery: read and applied the verified installed debugging and verification instructions; changed no plugin configuration or authentication.

# Mistake: routed two coordination messages to the wrong triage worker

- What the issue/mistake was: Initial follow-up messages assigned Monaco context to the OpenCode worker and SSH coordination to the Monaco worker.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed parallel wrapper results preserved dispatch ordering instead of confirming each worker's task ownership.
- How you fixed it: Confirmed ownership from worker reports and sent corrected coordination messages before edits overlapped.

# Mistake: release test invocation used the wrong working directory and Bun flag placement

- What the issue/mistake was: A release Vitest run used `--root` from the other checkout, producing a mismatched jest-dom matcher environment; a retry used unsupported `bun --cwd ... x` syntax.
- RCA of the Issue: I treated Vite's root option as equivalent to running from the target dependency/configuration directory and assumed Bun's flag ordering.
- How you fixed it: Ran Vitest from the release checkout's actual working directory with its installed dependencies; the focused activity tests passed 5/5.

# Mistake: synthetic Meraki regression leaked an importable helper module

- What the issue/mistake was: The new test called `install_meraki`, which replaced `sys.modules['meraki_api']` without initially restoring the prior module or its absence.
- RCA of the Issue: I isolated HTTP/session state but overlooked the installer's global import registration.
- How you fixed it: Registered the module change with pytest monkeypatch before installation so teardown restores the original state in both checkouts; reran focused tests and review.

# Mistake: portable smoke probe resolved the venv interpreter to system Python

- What the issue/mistake was: The smoke probe followed the venv Python symlink, losing its dependency environment.
- RCA of the Issue: I treated a venv symlink as equivalent to the portable runtime's real Python executable.
- How you fixed it: Added the known venv site-packages only to the disposable probe's Python path; real bundled runtime behavior and production code were unchanged.

# Mistake: cursor regression mock inferred a zero-argument create signature

- What the issue/mistake was: A new cursor assertion indexed the mocked editor creation options, but the mock's inferred zero-argument signature introduced TS2493.
- RCA of the Issue: The test ran successfully in Vitest while its inferred mock-call tuple had not been checked by TypeScript.
- How you fixed it: Typed the mock creation/configuration callback arguments and reran TypeScript diagnostics for owned files and focused tests; unrelated existing type errors were left untouched.

# Mistake: isolated cursor browser probe used mismatched module and fixture assumptions

- What the issue/mistake was: The first browser mount assumed a named React root export and later mixed Vite HMR-versioned provider modules; a new fixture also omitted the buffer ID expected by the registry mock. A verification wrapper used `tail` instead of the dedicated output reader.
- RCA of the Issue: I reused generic browser-module/fixture conventions before checking this editor's actual exports and model ownership contract, then took a shell-output shortcut.
- How you fixed it: Used version-matched imports and `bufferId='buffer'`, measured Windows-mode cursor behavior, and did not claim the failed post-edit macOS remount as verified. Use dedicated Read for further output inspection; no production source was changed by the failed probes.

# Mistake: Windows SSH prototype trusted corroboration without input-ownership proof

- What the issue/mistake was: Drafted a write-capable Windows verifier using console membership, SSH process identity, OSC-derived activity, and prompt shape. These signals do not establish who consumes PTY input or whether SSH authentication completed.
- RCA of the Issue: Treated process/lifecycle corroboration as equivalent to OS-owned foreground and authenticated-transport evidence. Remote output can forge OSC 133 state; background SSH can share a console; an MFA challenge can resemble a device prompt. Synthetic GREEN tests did not prove these security properties.
- How you fixed it: Independent Rust/security review blocked the draft before deployment. Withdrew only our prototype changes; the terminal-agent manager/gateway match their original baseline and the new native helper is gone. Retained the fail-closed Windows boundary with an actionable limitation, leaving macOS validation unchanged. Future execution support requires an app-owned authenticated transport with exclusive input ownership, not more prompt denylists. No real-device commands, credentials, or native Windows execution occurred.

# Mistake: swapped repository baseline claims during release-gate diagnosis

- What the issue/mistake was: A resolver's initial reports incorrectly attributed the scoped Kanban borrow fix to origin and the old `drop(state)` call to the public release checkout, making the origin Clippy log appear inconsistent.
- RCA of the Issue: Initial read results were not reconciled with explicit absolute `git -C` HEAD checks and the crate path in the compiler log. No mixed-output or stale-cache cause was established.
- How you fixed it: Verified origin HEAD `d876c06` contains the old call and public HEAD `2a48746` already contains the repair. Ported only the established public DictationService and Kanban lint fixes to origin, obtained Rust review, and reran the actual strict gates before publication. The public checkout required neither change.

# Verification finding: transient full-suite frontend failure during release preparation

- What the issue/mistake was: The first concurrent origin frontend run failed the unchanged CommandBlock rapid-click test (two invocations instead of one); it must not be silently counted as a passing run.
- RCA of the Issue: The precise transient mechanism was not established. The test uses a delayed mock, but timing was not proven as the cause.
- How you fixed it: Made no production/test changes or weakened assertions. A fresh full serial run passed all 2,255 tests; retained both results and the limitation in the session record.

# Verification finding: v1.1.8 caret mitigation failed native Windows acceptance

- What the issue/mistake was: The operator still cannot see the Monaco caret on Windows after v1.1.8. The solid-blink mitigation passed mocked tests and a Chromium Win32 override, but those checks did not reproduce the actual WebView2 failure.
- RCA of the Issue: The blink mitigation addressed timing without proving caret paint. Later native evidence shows focused input and a visible 2×19 cursor element with transparent background, missing theme variable, and inline-style CSP violations. Tauri adds a style nonce that defeats the intended unsafe-inline allowance; Monaco generates un-nonced theme CSS. An isolated real-Monaco 0.52.2 regression reproduces missing paint under that policy and proves an attribute-only correction is insufficient.
- How you fixed it: Prepared a Windows-only CSP override with separate attribute/element CSS allowances and the exact missing Windows IPC origin; scripts and the shared/macOS policy remain unchanged. Parent browser regression restores Zed cursor paint and preserves focus/blur and script denial, privacy checks pass, 27 editor tests and macOS all-targets cargo check pass. Native theme-sheet evidence and acceptance of a Windows build remain pending; no commit or release is claimed.

# Mistake: guessed store directory during Windows attachment triage

- What the issue/mistake was: The read-only SSH worker probed nonexistent `src/stores` instead of the actual state-store directory.
- RCA of the Issue: Inferred a conventional directory rather than locating the store first.
- How you fixed it: Located `src/state/terminalConnectionStore.ts` and traced actual callers. No source edits, credentials, or device access occurred.

# Mistake: proposed a different SSH experience and an unproven exec transport

- What the issue/mistake was: Proposed Windows diagnostics through a separate saved-target SSH connection instead of the user's expected same visible terminal. The initial transport sketch used SSH exec even though existing device code documents IOS/IOS-XE rejecting exec channels.
- RCA of the Issue: Treated existing one-shot/SFTP transport patterns as sufficient for interactive device compatibility and presented a workaround as the principal architecture before confirming the requested same-window behavior.
- How you fixed it: The operator clarified the required shared read/write terminal experience; independent security review caught the exec-channel mismatch before implementation. Withdrew the separate-source proposal and redirected design to an app-owned authenticated PTY/shell session in the same visible pane, with explicit reconnect for unmanaged sessions if needed. No production implementation, native acceptance, or commit is claimed.

# Mistake: malformed read-only regex during Windows security follow-up

- What the issue/mistake was: The security reviewer's first follow-up search used a malformed regex and failed.
- RCA of the Issue: The search pattern was not valid for the regex parser; no code finding can be inferred from that failed search.
- How you fixed it: Corrected the pattern and confirmed the actual Windows launcher and ConPTY locations. No source or runtime state was changed by the failed probe.

# Mistake: overstated macOS SSH binding as authentication verification

- What the issue/mistake was: Explained the Windows attachment gap as though the macOS session binding also proved SSH authentication succeeded. The actual macOS checks bind the saved connection to a matching foreground SSH process group; the frontend marks connected on command start, not authentication success.
- RCA of the Issue: Conflated the desired authenticated-input security property with what the current implementation actually verifies, without first tracing the macOS launcher, validator, and frontend lifecycle together.
- How you fixed it: A focused Rust source comparison established the actual contract, and I corrected the explanation to the operator. Existing nonce/lease/capture machinery can be reused for a Windows-native, explicitly launched OpenSSH child in an app-owned ConPTY; process ownership and authentication must remain separate. Native behavior, safe client configuration, and any trusted authentication signal remain unverified. No production fix, manual-session adoption, or commit is claimed.

# Mistake: guessed catalog-index shape in the Kanban bootstrap regression

- What the issue/mistake was: The first test used nonexistent `active_catalog_ids`, then assumed the filtered catalog index could never be `None`.
- RCA of the Issue: Inferred an attribute from a builder argument and overlooked the documented empty-index result after unconfigured vendors are filtered.
- How you fixed it: Read the real index and grounding implementation, used `catalog_ids`, and accepted `None` or absence of Meraki after first proving the synthetic merged Meraki catalog exists. The corrected real-graph bootstrap regression passes.

# Mistake: assumed optional config readers never attempted an absent database

- What the issue/mistake was: A synthetic bootstrap test initially expected zero intercepted database-open attempts; the optional Zabbix config reader tries opening an absent config DB.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed every optional reader checks file existence first rather than tracing the actual reader.
- How you fixed it: Kept all database operations intercepted before I/O, asserted any attempted path stays under the synthetic HOME, and returned an absent-database error. Operator-database and network attempts still fail the test; no real database or credentials were read.

# Mistake: initial bootstrap regression bypassed the graph and leaked installer state

- What the issue/mistake was: The first fixture substituted a dictionary for the graph constructor, replaced the Python environment mapping rather than the native environment before runtime imports, and did not restore sandbox helper registrations.
- RCA of the Issue: Focused on vendor selection while overlooking the actual graph factory and global environment/module side effects.
- How you fixed it: Used the real graph factory with captured arguments, native environment monkeypatching and SQLite/socket/request guards before runtime imports, and a scoped helper-registration finalizer. Independent reruns passed six focused tests and a teardown canary proving original module identities/absences and environment restoration. Earlier executed probes already used an externally isolated interpreter/HOME; the standalone test now enforces its own isolation. This proves synthetic bootstrap, not live LLM research.

# Mistake: caret diagnostic could sample the wrong pane and duplicate listeners

- What the issue/mistake was: The first collector fell back to another pane's Monaco instance when the focused editor was unavailable; repeated installation also accumulated CSP listeners.
- RCA of the Issue: Treated any editor as representative of the focused pane and did not account for reinstalling the probe during editor-mode changes.
- How you fixed it: Removed the arbitrary fallback, report focused-editor unavailability, and dispose prior editor/window listeners before reinstalling. The persistent regression and independent review verify one CSP report, no old-editor output, and no exported private markers. No production caret fix or native acceptance is claimed.

# Mistake: initial caret-probe check assumed jsdom layout and event ordering

- What the issue/mistake was: A one-off diagnostic test assumed native caret width and an event order not established by its fixture.
- RCA of the Issue: Confused jsdom's synthetic geometry with a native rendered layout and relied on implicit events.
- How you fixed it: Corrected the test invocation, then left a runnable check with explicit event dispatch and only declared fields, focus/composition, listener lifecycle, and privacy assertions. Native geometry remains evidence to collect on Windows, not a mocked passing claim.

# Mistake: described working SSH as failing and did not deliver caret directions

- What the issue/mistake was: Asked how the failing SSH session was opened even though the reported failure was agent attachment to a working session, and referred to local diagnostic files rather than providing usable Windows directions.
- RCA of the Issue: Conflated SSH connectivity with the attachment trust boundary and assumed the operator could access uncommitted local scripts. Promised the probe later instead of delivering it.
- How you fixed it: Recorded the reported Windows OpenSSH 9.5p2 version, corrected the failure boundary, and prepared direct copy-paste PowerShell/DevTools instructions requiring no clone or build. The launcher obtains the path from the running app and uses Edge's local inspection page; syntax and focused privacy checks pass, while native execution remains pending.

# Mistake: repeated cached reads and incomplete first-touch facts

- What the issue/mistake was: Re-requested unchanged diagnostic files already in context, then attempted first Bash/Edit operations without all hook-required request, consumer, API, and data facts. Repeated the first-touch omission for this Markdown log after compaction, the first Bash command after receiving native console evidence, and a log edit that relied on an earlier rather than fresh consumer search. Also attempted Windows override creation before demonstrating that no alternate platform configuration served the same purpose.
- RCA of the Issue: Treated previously established context as sufficient for a new first-touch gate and did not use the current cached file state.
- How you fixed it: Reused the known source state, searched consumers, presented the exact user request and diagnostic-only/no-persistent-data contract, then completed the permitted edits. No hook exemptions, permission changes, or bypasses were used.
- Recurrence during v1.1.9 publication preparation: First Bash, temporary scope-manifest creation, Markdown edits, and the diagnostic version-confirmation edit were rejected because I omitted freshly required explicit facts, the complete consumer search, or the exact target path in the facts. I searched tracked references and local guidance, presented the current instruction, identified exact targets, consumers and public API impact, described the path-array and Markdown schemas or lack of schema changes, then retried the permitted operations without exemptions.
- Recurrence after native release completion (2026-10-07): Initial verification Bash and ledger Edit calls on background resumption were rejected because I relied on earlier or incomplete facts. Re-stated the verbatim current request, exact command purpose, complete documentation references, public API impact, and Markdown/date structure before permitted retries without exemptions. Rejected attempts changed no files or downloads.
- Recurrence during the remaining ownership-error follow-up (2026-10-07): Initial inventory Bash and ledger Edit calls were rejected despite surrounding request/purpose or reference facts; those presentations did not satisfy the gate. Re-presented the current request, exact purpose, import/require versus documentation consumers, public API impact, and explicit Markdown field names before permitted retries. No exemptions, source changes, or hidden permission workarounds were used.

# Mistake: caret launcher omitted build confirmation and Edge-target cleanup

- What the issue/mistake was: Auto-detecting TerminAI.exe did not establish that the installed build was v1.1.8, and the Edge inspection directions did not explain removing the target added for the probe.
- RCA of the Issue: Verified the executable filename and debugger listener but overlooked explicit build provenance and the inspection page's retained configuration.
- How you fixed it: Added a local executable-path display and explicit v1.1.8 confirmation before relaunching, plus instructions to remove only the Edge target added for this session. The app is never forcibly closed and native execution is still pending.

# Mistake: malformed Python quotation in read-only CSP version probe

- What the issue/mistake was: The CSP reviewer’s first Cargo.lock version probe failed with Python SyntaxError.
- RCA of the Issue: Escaped quotation marks inside an f-string expression in a shell-quoted one-liner.
- How you fixed it: The reviewer reran the probe with str.format and obtained the locked Tauri versions. No files changed and no configuration or credentials were read.

# Mistake: guessed editor paths during CSP triage

- What the issue/mistake was: The CSP reviewer’s editor search returned rg exit 2 for five nonexistent paths directly under src/components/.
- RCA of the Issue: Searched conventional locations before inventorying this repository’s component paths.
- How you fixed it: Located the editors with rg --files under src/components/editor/ and src/components/iac/, then searched those actual paths. No files changed.

# Mistake: Monaco CSP fixture assumed POSIX output paths

- What the issue/mistake was: The first real-Monaco browser fixture matched esbuild outputs by /fixture.js and /fixture.css suffixes, which could reject Windows paths before running the test.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed the macOS host’s path separators represented every test platform.
- How you fixed it: Normalize separators before basename matching. The current source-backed three-policy browser regression passes; native Windows execution is not claimed.

# Mistake: loopback server was treated as complete fixture network isolation

- What the issue/mistake was: The real-Monaco fixture served only loopback assets but had no explicit browser-request allowlist.
- RCA of the Issue: Conflated local-only serving with restricting requests initiated by the browser page. No external request or operator-data exposure was demonstrated.
- How you fixed it: Install a context route before page creation, continuing only the fixture origin and aborting every other request without logging URLs. Parent independently reran all three policy cases and the privacy collector check.

# Mistake: browser shutdown error could skip fixture server cleanup

- What the issue/mistake was: Sequential awaits in the outer finally could skip server.close if browser.close rejected.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed the first cleanup operation could not fail, instead of guaranteeing cleanup of both owned resources.
- How you fixed it: Nested server.close in its own finally. Parent independently reran the real-Monaco browser regression successfully; final review remains separate from native WebView2 acceptance.

# Mistake: Windows managed-SSH dependency omitted its lockfile edge

- What the issue/mistake was: Added the Windows-only `windows-sys` dependency to `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` without updating `Cargo.lock`; the parent's `cargo check --locked --all-targets` stopped before compilation because it could not update the lockfile.
- RCA of the Issue: I deferred Cargo to avoid concurrent runs but failed to coordinate an offline lockfile synchronization before handing off the manifest change.
- How you fixed it: With exclusive Cargo ownership, ran `cargo update --offline --workspace --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, adding only the root `windows-sys 0.61.2` lockfile dependency edge and preserving all package versions. `cargo check --locked --offline --all-targets` then passed; native Windows authentication behavior remains unverified.

# Mistake: Windows managed-SSH stale Exit could revoke replacement authority

- What the issue/mistake was: An old PTY Exit passed the generation check, then a replacement registered before the old event performed PTY-ID-scoped lease/binding/capture cleanup.
- RCA of the Issue: The PTY check and manager cleanup held different locks at different times; a generation check before cleanup was not an atomic authority boundary.
- How you fixed it: Scoped managed events to the binding generation under the manager's lease → binding locks; held the spawn gate from every Exit validation through frontend delivery. The regression exercises a late old Exit after replacement and verifies the new lease, binding, and capture survive. Rust tests still require the parent's exclusive Cargo run; native Windows remains unverified.

# Mistake: concurrent PTY spawns could overwrite one preferred tab id

- What the issue/mistake was: Two concurrent restores could pass the post-spawn duplicate-ID check separately and overwrite the first PTY, leaving its SSH child untracked.
- RCA of the Issue: Check and insertion were separate PTY-map lock acquisitions, with no reservation across asynchronous spawn.
- How you fixed it: Serialized the check/spawn/register sequence with an async spawn gate and added an atomic check-and-insert helper that returns the unused child for killing. Synthetic concurrent-spawn and collision regressions were added; Cargo execution is pending parent ownership.

# Mistake: managed-SSH lease captured a connection id but not the verified child

- What the issue/mistake was: An old attachment could be issued after takeover or reused against a new authenticated SSH process under the same tab and connection id.
- RCA of the Issue: Validation checked current PID/generation, but the lease stored only serializable PTY/connection metadata; gateway writes re-checked a freshly fetched current child, not the one validated for the lease.
- How you fixed it: Stored the verified generation, PID, and takeover epoch privately in the lease; made issuance compare the validated snapshot under the lease → binding locks, and required that exact child for every initial and pager write. Added validation/takeover/replacement regressions and zero-write assertions; Cargo execution is pending parent ownership.

# Mistake: Monaco caret fixture asserted before async cursor paint

- What the issue/mistake was: The first real-typing fixture assertion read the DOM caret before Monaco had painted the updated cursor.
- RCA of the Issue: The fixture assumed typing and the next browser paint completed synchronously.
- How you fixed it: The fixture owner waited for rendered cursor movement before asserting; three consecutive synthetic fixture runs passed. No production code changed for this correction.

# Mistake: isolated Windows helper check used the wrong Rust toolchain

- What the issue/mistake was: A temporary-directory Windows MSVC helper check initially failed E0463 (missing `core`) despite the project-scoped Windows target being installed.
- RCA of the Issue: The temporary working directory did not inherit the repository's Rust toolchain override and selected the default Rust 1.95 toolchain rather than the installed Rust 1.97 toolchain with that target.
- How you fixed it: The helper-check owner pinned the installed Rust 1.97 Cargo/RUSTC paths explicitly; isolated all-targets Windows helper type-check then passed offline with cached dependencies. This does not prove the full application or native behavior, and modified helper source needs rechecking.

# Mistake: managed-SSH restore marker was not persisted when reusing a tab id

- What the issue/mistake was: The first provenance change supplied a marker to `create_tab_with_id`, but an existing row retained its old executable `shell_cmd` during the upsert.
- RCA of the Issue: I assumed the idempotent tab-reopen helper updated shell metadata; its conflict branch only clears `closed_at`.
- How you fixed it: Explicitly persist the exact Windows managed-SSH marker in the existing tab row after the upsert; a synthetic in-memory SQLite test covers an old executable shell being replaced by the disconnected marker. Rust tests await the parent's Cargo run.

# Mistake: PTY output could cross a reopened tab generation

- What the issue/mistake was: Non-Exit output passed the PTY generation check before a replacement and was later persisted/emitted under the reused tab id.
- RCA of the Issue: The spawn lifecycle gate only covered Exit, not the full event check-to-delivery interval for output.
- How you fixed it: Hold the spawn lifecycle gate for every event through generation check, manager capture, persistence, and frontend delivery; added a synthetic output/replacement interleaving regression. Rust verification awaits the parent's Cargo run.

# Mistake: close during pending spawn could strand an SSH child

- What the issue/mistake was: `pty_kill` could run after spawn's occupancy check but before child registration, close the tab with no handle, then let spawn register a live, untracked child.
- RCA of the Issue: `pty_kill` did not use the same spawn lifecycle gate as check/spawn/registration.
- How you fixed it: Make `pty_kill` await the shared gate through revocation, map removal, and tab close. For managed SSH, keep the gate through bounded, confirmed child termination, restoring the tracked handle and leaving the tab open on failure; the waiter records successful `child.wait()` before it sends Exit, so this does not await event-loop delivery. A synthetic close-during-pending-spawn regression was added; final Rust verification awaits the parent's Cargo rerun.

# Mistake: frontend restore dropped a resolved PTY before explicit-close cleanup

- What the issue/mistake was: An initial exit cleared the resolved PTY promise, so later explicit close could no longer use it to finish cleanup.
- RCA of the Issue: Restore state treated initial exit as permission to discard the identity still needed by explicit close.
- How you fixed it: The frontend owner retained the resolved promise, blocked input, and added a close-cleanup regression. This note records their reported correction; backend did not modify frontend restore code.

# Mistake: formatter check loop shadowed zsh's PATH array

- What the issue/mistake was: A formatting-check loop lost access to `rg` and `git` and returned an unusable result.
- RCA of the Issue: In zsh, assigning the loop variable `path` also changes the tied `PATH` array.
- How you fixed it: Reran the checks with `target_file` as the loop variable; remaining rustfmt differences matched the pre-existing HEAD formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.

# Mistake: managed detach dropped the live connection when history carried a restore marker

- What the issue/mistake was: Detaching a live managed SSH tab lost its connection rather than moving the existing wire session.
- RCA of the Issue: Detach eligibility checked only terminal type and split count; the detached renderer saw the managed-history marker and skipped `wireSession`.
- How you fixed it: The frontend owner now shares the `isManagedHistory`/live predicate across button, drag, and handler guards. Live managed detach remains intentionally unsupported without a replacement; ordinary macOS/local detach is untouched. This records the frontend owner's reported fix, not a backend edit.

# Mistake: managed close discarded a child before confirming termination

- What the issue/mistake was: `pty_kill` removed the tracked child, released the lifecycle gate, ignored kill failure, and closed the tab before the child was known to have exited. A new PTY could reuse the same tab id while the old SSH child still ran.
- RCA of the Issue: Sending a kill request is not evidence of successful `child.wait()`, and the existing `alive` flag deliberately becomes false even on an unknown wait outcome, so it cannot certify exit.
- How you fixed it: Managed Windows close retains the gate while requesting termination and waiting (bounded) for a separate confirmed-success wait flag; on unconfirmed termination (including actual kill failure, wait failure, or timeout) it restores the handle, leaves the tab open, and surfaces an error. The waiter still marks unknown outcomes not alive for fail-closed lease checks. Added synthetic failure/timeout guards and a controlled local-process termination test; parent Cargo rerun and native Windows verification remain pending.

# Mistake: wait-state observation could misclassify a successful exit as failure

- What the issue/mistake was: Managed close could report an unknown/failed child wait even though a successful exit had just been confirmed.
- RCA of the Issue: The close loop read `confirmed == false` before the waiter stored `true`, then read `alive == false` after the waiter cleared it. Two separate atomic observations straddled the state transition.
- How you fixed it: Recheck confirmation after observing `alive == false`, both in the wait loop and the initial no-longer-alive path. A deterministic callback regression reproduces the interleaving; genuinely failed waits remain unconfirmed and fail closed.

# Mistake: frontend close discarded a managed tab after native kill failed

- What the issue/mistake was: The UI removed a managed terminal tab even when its native PTY close failed, losing the operator's ability to retry termination while the SSH child might still be running.
- RCA of the Issue: TabBar used a void registry disposal that swallowed `ptyKill` rejection before removing the tab; Pane split-close also used void disposal before closing the pane.
- How you fixed it: The frontend owner now awaits native kill for managed-tab close and retains the tab with an error/retry path on rejection; managed root split close and Cmd-W are guarded, while local split-close behavior is unchanged. This records the frontend owner's report, not a backend edit.

# Mistake: dead managed SSH remained an eligible pipe target

- What the issue/mistake was: A naturally exited managed SSH process left its historical PTY handle in the map, and the PipeMenu could still offer that dead tab as a target.
- RCA of the Issue: `list_pipe_targets` treated map membership alone as liveness; managed SSH handles can remain mapped after Exit for history/explicit close.
- How you fixed it: Filter only dead managed SSH handles using the actual handle's liveness while preserving ordinary/macOS map-membership semantics. A synthetic shared-predicate regression covers live/dead managed and ordinary combinations; Rust verification awaits the parent's Cargo rerun.

# Mistake: Windows PTY kill error was mistaken for proof termination failed

- What the issue/mistake was: Managed close could refuse to close a successfully terminated SSH child because its kill request reported an error.
- RCA of the Issue: In the locked `portable-pty` 0.9.0 Windows implementation, `WinChildKiller::kill` has an inverted `TerminateProcess` result check and can return Err on successful termination; the first close helper returned on Err before consulting the independent waiter.
- How you fixed it: Always await bounded confirmed-success `child.wait()` after requesting managed termination, whether the kill call reports Ok or Err. Confirmed exit wins; otherwise keep the handle tracked and include any kill error in the actionable failure. Deterministic tests cover false kill Err with confirmed exit, real failure, and no-exit timeout. No dependency or ordinary macOS behavior changed; parent Cargo rerun/native Windows runtime verification remain pending.

# Mistake: Windows auth callback could treat a digit-ending nonce as redirection

- What the issue/mistake was: A generated UUID ending in a digit could be shortened in the local callback, and a nonce fragment could reach terminal output instead of the authentication pipe.
- RCA of the Issue: The callback placed `>` immediately after the expanded environment variable. Windows cmd.exe treats a trailing digit before `>` as a file-descriptor redirect; the pipe then receives a malformed nonce. This command parsing behavior was confirmed against Microsoft documentation, not device output.
- How you fixed it: Put the pipe redirection before `echo` in the fixed LocalCommand, leaving nonce out of argv and retaining the exact 36-byte UUID plus CRLF proof gate. A synthetic helper test asserts the prefix and several digit-ending UUID shapes. This is not a native cmd.exe execution test; parent Cargo/helper check and Windows runtime verification remain pending.

# Mistake: guessed public release documentation during candidate transplant

- What the issue/mistake was: The public transplant worker included a nonexistent `docs/RELEASING.md` in a read-only consumer search, producing an exit-2 result after valid matches.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed a conventional documentation name rather than using the already identified release procedure.
- How you fixed it: Used the existing release preflight and changed-Rust formatting scripts already read. No source changes or findings were lost; this records the worker's report.

# Mistake: public patch-fidelity probe assumed development hunk offsets

- What the issue/mistake was: The transplant worker's in-memory fidelity check asserted at an AI-command hunk even though Git had applied the reviewed patch cleanly.
- RCA of the Issue: Used development patch line numbers as public-base positions despite the seven known committed-base differences, instead of Git's context matching.
- How you fixed it: Replaced the offset-based probe with actual Git patch application in a disposable baseline fixture and comparison of formatted expected contents. No public worktree source changed and no functional gate failed; this records the worker's reported recovery.

# Mistake: managed SSH validator failed Windows strict Clippy

- What the issue/mistake was: Public CI 37568910095 rejected the v1.1.9 candidate for unused `foreground_identity` and an overwritten initial `managed_identity` assignment in `commands/ai.rs`; Windows test compilation did not run.
- RCA of the Issue: The foreground-process probe is consumed only by the non-Windows path, while every successful Windows branch assigns its managed identity. Native macOS checks did not compile these Windows-specific warning paths.
- How you fixed it: The Rust resolver gated the Unix probe to non-Windows and used definite initialization for the Windows identity in both source trees. Authentication, focus, pinned ownership, lease, and gateway checks remain unchanged; no lint suppression or release-gate change. Rust and final scope reviewers approved with zero findings. Parent strict all-targets Clippy passed in both candidates; development library 608 passed / 6 ignored, public library 622 passed / 6 ignored, and both terminal-agent contract suites passed 12/12. Formatting, whitespace, release preflight, and graph refresh passed. Native Windows acceptance remains unverified; the release tag remains blocked until corrected Windows CI succeeds.

# Mistake: verification tooling attempted ineffective edit and out-of-range read

- What the issue/mistake was: The resolver attempted a no-op Edit, which the tool rejected; the parent requested release-validator lines starting at 330 in a 152-line file.
- RCA of the Issue: The proposed replacement did not change content, and the read offset assumed a file length not established by evidence.
- How you fixed it: The resolver applied the actual minimal replacements; the parent read the short validator normally. Neither failed operation changed source or weakened verification.

# Mistake: post-publication verifier assumed browser-only updater URLs

- What the issue/mistake was: The first local artifact-verification script raised KeyError on a legitimate GitHub API asset URL before checking the Windows installer checksum.
- RCA of the Issue: The ad hoc URL map indexed only `browser_download_url`, although the existing release validator explicitly supports both `url` and `browser_download_url`.
- How you fixed it: Indexed both supported fields without modifying published assets or release gates, then verified exact changelog/updater notes, all six updater asset/signature records, Windows signature-file consistency, installer size, and installer SHA-256 against both SHA256SUMS.txt and GitHub's asset digest. Native GUI/device acceptance and local cryptographic signature verification were not performed.

# Mistake: released attachment helper retained the pre-managed Windows downgrade

- What the issue/mistake was: The operator reports v1.1.9 fixes the other Windows issues but Architect terminal attachment still produces the ownership error. The public attachment helper converts even a connected managed Windows session to `manual_ssh` and omits its saved connection identity.
- RCA of the Issue: The public-only `canVerifySavedSshBinding` predicate still allowed macOS only and was outside the transplanted candidate files. The regression covered unmanaged Windows fallback, not the newly supported managed launch-to-attachment route. # Mistake: concurrent terminal diagnostics could outrun investigation-plan setup

- What the issue/mistake was: On 2026-10-07, the Windows operator's attached Architect turn batched begin-investigation and three diagnostics; a diagnostic raised the local missing-plan ValueError and aborted the entire DeepAgents turn. The supplied traceback confirmed the sidecar guard, not the previous SSH ownership rejection.
- RCA of the Issue: LangGraph executes sibling tools concurrently. Plan readiness was set only after the gateway returned, while diagnostics checked readiness before the existing command lock. That lock covered commands but not plan creation/publication. Expected prerequisite rejection was an unhandled execution ValueError instead of a model-visible tool error. Earlier tests only ran parallel diagnostics after a completed plan.
- How you fixed it: In public and development source, reused the existing lock through plan acceptance and plan-event publication before setting readiness; moved the diagnostic prerequisite inside that lock. Only the diagnostic's expected missing-plan guard now raises handled ToolException; unrelated gateway failures and update/fix guards remain unchanged. Parent reproduced the exact ValueError against immutable public HEAD loaded in memory using one compiled-graph tool batch, without replacing worktree source. The final patch passed 91/91 focused tests in each checkout and 12/12 terminal tests on each of three parent repeat runs, with isolated HOME/database paths and tracing disabled. Code/Python/security reviewers found no remaining findings. Rust ownership, captured visible SSH identity, lease, Tier-0 and exact fix-approval checks were not changed. Graph refreshed; raw RemoteLogs remained ignored and untracked. No real device command, new build, commit, push or release was run; native Windows end-to-end acceptance remains pending.

# Mistake: first race tests inferred contention from a short timeout

Failed-plan testing also uses entry events, with release in finally. Parent independently verified red against immutable HEAD and green plus repeats; both reviewers closed the finding by inspecting the final tests.

# Mistake: macOS pre-bundle signing hook used Windows-unsafe inline JavaScript

- What the issue/mistake was: On 2026-10-15, the v1.1.11 Windows release executable built successfully, but the newly added `bun -e "if (...) ..."` beforeBundleCommand failed on all three attempts with an opening literal quote and Bun's `Unterminated string literal`. Windows packaging failed and publication was correctly skipped; macOS and Linux passed.
- RCA of the Issue: Put the macOS platform check inside shell-quoted inline JavaScript, so Windows had to parse the problematic command even though signing should be skipped there. The original regression evaluated already-normalized JavaScript arguments and ran the shell path only on the host, missing the Windows quoting boundary. A local quote-preserving argv probe can also turn the old payload into a harmless string expression rather than reproduce cmd.exe exactly; simulated platform tests must not be described as native Windows verification.
- How you fixed it: Prepared an uncommitted three-file public-worktree repair: quote-free `bun scripts/sign-sidecar-macos.mjs`, a small object-oriented launcher that returns immediately outside Darwin and delegates to the unchanged signing shell helper on Darwin, and standalone cross-platform hook regressions. Spawn errors and missing/signal exit status fail closed. Four focused tests were red against the old command and green after the change; all 40 release-helper tests, no-Git manifest preflight, JavaScript syntax, and whitespace checks passed locally on macOS with Bun 1.3.6. Native Windows packaging remains unverified. No commit, push, tag move, workflow rerun, or manual publication was performed. The original checkout's unrelated changes remain intact.
- Gate-recovery recurrence: Initial Bash/test Edit/new launcher Write/config Edit/private ledger Edit attempts were rejected before execution. Re-presented the ongoing approval verbatim, searched the existing-purpose/caller references, and expanded the required facts into individual numbered caller/API-or-creation/schema/request entries before successful retries. No exemptions or permission changes were used; these blocked calls did not change files or remote state.
- Regression-count precision: The red run executed four methods; the host-shell method passed, while four assertion/subtest failures occurred across the other three methods. All four methods passed after repair. This was not native Windows execution.
