# Mistake: API-update first-touch fact presentation repeatedly failed the gate (2026-10-16)

- What the issue/mistake was: Twenty initial file-touch calls were rejected during this implementation, including the user-guide edit and local graph-refresh script creation; none of those rejected calls applied.
- RCA of the Issue: Supplied abbreviated purpose/context facts rather than the gate's complete target-specific importer/caller, public-API, data-contract and verbatim-current-instruction facts.
- How you fixed it: Verified callers/contracts and retried with the full explicit facts, without exemptions, permission changes or delegating blocked work. Kept execution evidence and preserved all unrelated working-tree edits. Context7 documentation remained unavailable pending authentication; no OAuth/configuration change or successful Context7 lookup is claimed.
- Live-ISE-test recurrence (2026-10-16): The first read-only Bash check, first new harness Write and first ledger Edit were rejected before execution. RCA: abbreviated facts omitted the complete numbered command-purpose, absolute-path consumer, public-API and synthetic data/date contract presentation. Re-presented the user's exact live-test request and command purpose, searched existing source/test callers, supplied the complete facts and retried without exemptions or permission changes. The corrected harness invokes the real ISE start/status RPCs, snapshots the other ten product catalogs and displays no credentials or agent prompts. Rejected calls changed no files or installed catalogs.
- Live-harness evidence correction (2026-10-16): Review found the harness's zero-operation field was a static declaration rather than a measured counter, and a missing non-target file could pass unchanged as None. RCA: verification output overstated its measurement contract and did not require snapshot files to exist. Removed the counter claim, explicitly marked vendor execution as not independently instrumented, required every non-target file to exist, and retained inventory hashes in new reports. Preserved historical reports unchanged and saved a separate evidence clarification. Preflight had confirmed all11 installed catalogs; a fresh check confirmed10 non-target files and rejects missing files. The first missing-file probe used an incomplete SimpleNamespace without PRODUCTS and raised AttributeError; corrected it to use the real inspector with a temporary root. No production updater change or extra vendor execution occurred. Two real public ISE refreshes succeeded:414 additions, then0 with identical catalog bytes; existing data/backup and other products preserved.

# Mistake: API-update result wording did not distinguish counters from starting coverage (2026-10-16)

- What the issue/mistake was: Reported “414 added,17 already known” without explaining that already_known counts matching candidates during publication, not the number of operations in the pre-update catalog. This also left “additions” open to interpretation as entirely new product features.
- RCA of the Issue: Repeated implementation counter names rather than measuring and explaining the original catalog's effective records. The publication loop includes newly accepted records in subsequent overlap checks and deliberately preserves meaningful trailing slashes.
- How you fixed it: Compared the actual installed catalog with its managed pre-update backup and ran the real CatalogIndex on that backup. Verified one original generic function exposing22 parsed method/path records;414 appended records comprise339 ERS,60 OpenAPI and15 MnT operations, with188 GET,84 PUT,69 POST,48 DELETE and25 PATCH. Ten additions are trailing-slash variants of original method/path records. Clarified that17 is the run's matching-candidate count, not starting coverage;414 is catalog records added, not414 distinct new or appliance-verified features. No catalog or production-code changes.

# Mistake: AI canvas discovery setup and research routing (2026-10-20)

- What the issue/mistake was: The initial read-only skill/status command and first mistakes-log edit were rejected before execution because their required fact presentations were incomplete. A library-audit update intended for the repository explorer was also sent to the Cisco product researcher.
- RCA of the Issue: Relied on conversational setup rather than explicit command/file-contract facts and the verbatim instruction, and associated concurrently returned agent IDs with invocation order rather than verifying their task roles.
- How you fixed it: Presented the request and command purpose, then all four file facts, and retried without changing permissions. Used ListAgents to verify actual roles, sent the update to the explorer, and clarified the Cisco-only assignment with the researcher. No product implementation, scope approval, or commit occurred. Verify recipient roles before cross-agent handoffs and present complete first-touch facts before edits.
- Mockup recurrence: After the user approved browser mockups, the initial server-launch, new-HTML and ledger-edit calls were rejected before execution because the facts omitted the exact current-request framing, absolute reference paths, server consumer line numbers, or a duplicate-purpose search. Re-presented the complete facts and retried without exemptions. The disposable mockup initially let a grid item's automatic minimum height push the canvas footer over the page footer. Playwright geometry identified the canvas as 830px inside a 763px workspace; adding min-height:0 to that grid item fixed the root cause. Both layout variants then fit 1440x1000 and 1280x800 without page overflow or footer overlap. Interaction checks passed with no page errors or external requests; the first harness's static summary undercounted its assertions, so do not treat that label as an authoritative test count. All incident data is synthetic; no product code or device operations were involved.
- Layout-selection recurrence: The first static waiting-screen creation after the user selected B was rejected before execution. RCA: abbreviated facts did not explicitly enumerate the data-field/date-format non-applicability and current file-search evidence. Rechecked exact server consumer lines and the content directory, presented all four facts explicitly with the verbatim B instruction, and created the screen without permission changes. The original interactive mockup remains intact.
- Design-write recurrence: The first design-document creation after compaction and first follow-up ledger edit were rejected before execution. RCA: relied on summarized search evidence/earlier feature wording for the design and abbreviated reference paths/current instruction for the ledger, rather than a fresh complete numbered presentation. Repeated the searches, verified the new path did not exist, cited exact review-workflow consumers, explicitly stated public-function and data-schema non-applicability, and quoted the full current instruction. The design was created successfully; retried the ledger with complete facts without exemptions, permission changes or delegated bypass. No product implementation or commit occurred.
- Graph-query verification recurrence: A generic natural-language smoke query seeded unrelated common-word nodes rather than the newly indexed design. RCA: skipped the prescribed current-vocabulary/source qualification before traversal; a successful CLI exit does not establish relevant retrieval. Read the query guidance and verified the exact design source has all 28 expected nodes and 36 relationships instead. Confirmed graph self-loops are unchanged at four before/after. Do not cite the noisy query as successful design retrieval or treat inherited graph warnings as complete source coverage.
- Plan-write recurrence: The initial implementation-plan Write after continuation was rejected before execution. RCA: quoted the earlier “approved” design decision rather than the current verbatim continuation instruction and relied on summarized duplicate-search facts. Repeated the repository Markdown/target-path searches, explicitly distinguished workflow consumers from nonexistent runtime callers/public APIs and Markdown from application data, quoted the complete current instruction, and created the plan successfully. No hooks/permissions were changed, no denied write was delegated, and no implementation or commit occurred.
- Implementation-start recurrence: After Gate 1 approval the first read-only baseline/graph command was rejected before execution; its announcement did not explicitly enumerate both first-Bash facts. A grouped plan/log/ledger edit was then rejected because shared facts did not enumerate each target's full searched consumers and public-API non-applicability. Repeated reference/duplicate searches, presented complete target-specific facts, and retried each operation separately. Baseline capture and standalone ledger/plan updates succeeded without hooks/permission changes or delegated bypass. Preserve dirty-work originals and use target-specific facts before edits; staging, commits and live-device calls remain unauthorized.
- Continuation-bookkeeping recurrence: Requested an unnecessary reread of the unchanged ledger and had a status-only design edit rejected before execution. RCA: failed to rely on the restored Read result and gave abbreviated consumers/approval rather than all target-specific facts and the current verbatim continuation instruction. Fix: used the restored ledger state, enumerated exact searched consumers, explicit API/schema non-applicability and the verbatim instruction; the standalone status edit then succeeded. No hook/permission settings changed, no pending worker transcript was read, and no denied operation was delegated. A later first report creation repeated the abbreviated-instruction mistake and was also blocked before execution; providing all four separately numbered target-specific facts and the complete verbatim continuation instruction allowed the standalone retry. Do not abbreviate the instruction on first-touch writes.
- Task 1 Rust lifetime error: The implementer returned a rusqlite mapped-row expression whose temporary outlived the statement borrow, causing E0597. RCA: a tail-expression temporary retained the borrow across statement teardown. Fix: collect mapped results before returning; the implementer reports the focused Rust integration target subsequently passed 4/4. Independent code review is pending.
- Task 1 fixture initialization error: All four Rust tests failed when sqlite-vec was registered after opening SQLite. RCA: auto-extension registration must precede connection creation. Fix: register sqlite-vec first in the synthetic fixture; the implementer reports three final 4/4 runs. Do not confuse a fixture failure with production CRUD behavior.
- Task 1 stale-response race: A store race assertion failed before response-version handling was added. RCA: a stale asynchronous load could apply after newer view state. Fix: the implementer added request-version handling and reports final frontend 19/19; independent review must verify the actual race coverage.
- Task 1 Cargo scope/disk failure: Broad/name-filtered Cargo invocations built additional integration/static-library outputs and failed with `No space left on device (os error 28)`. Fix/workaround: use the explicit `--test canvas_local_cases` target, which the implementer reports passed. No artifacts deleted; full builds/regressions remain unverified and space is not claimed fixed. Controller later measured 4.9 GiB available.
- Task 1 Graphify scope error: The implementer ran `graphify update` against the repository root without recorded exclusions, contrary to the intended non-sensitive source scope. RCA: the generic graph-update instruction was not constrained to an explicit feature-owned allowlist. Containment/fix: a read-only metadata audit found 83 added/3 removed IDs (54 feature-source additions, 5 unrelated graph-memory-document nodes, 24 source-less). No added source path was sensitive-looking; source-less entries were not declared safe. Backed up the broad artifacts and atomically published JSON/report/aggregated HTML; exit 0, 45,812 nodes/141,379 edges. Four self-loops and 560 missing labels persist; total missing source_file is 3,371 including generated references. This is constrained graph navigation, not a full-source, security or execution guarantee; no exposed values used/reproduced.
- Scoped Graphify validator assumption: The first isolated extraction was rejected before graph publication because the validator treated every node source_file as a parsed input. RCA: Graphify also emits minimal import-reference stubs whose source_file is an extensionless module reference, not a scanned file. Fix: inspect only returned node metadata; keep the explicit input allowlist closed, preserve the 27 existing baseline reference records instead of replacing/pruning their attributes, and mark any genuinely new unresolved reference separately. The retry succeeded without opening referenced foreign files or broadening the source scan. No graph was overwritten by the failed attempt.
- Task 2 report fact-gate recurrence: The first report Write was rejected before execution despite a preamble with the full continuation instruction. No hook/permission bypass or delegated denial.
- Task 2 fixture ownership/identical-edit mistakes: A synthetic attachment ID was moved before reuse, causing compilation failure; RCA was Rust fixture ownership, fixed with a clone before observing the intended behavioral RED. An identical old/new Edit was rejected without changing a file; RCA was supplying the wrong replacement text, fixed by the intended test insertion. No production data or permission change.
- Task 2 build infrastructure failures: Even the narrow canvas_cards target failed exit 101 building libccie_terminal_lib.a with ENOSPC; canvas_local_cases separately failed exit 101 creating a temporary target/debug/deps directory with ENOENT. RCA at the observed layer is insufficient build-output space / absent target directory; why target availability changed is unknown and no actor is inferred. Both exact commands later passed (cards 6/local 5); neither failure is a behavioral test result. No artifact deletion by worker/controller. Parent later measured 27 GiB available, not proof narrowing itself fixed capacity.
- Task 2 fix-round-1 command/test mistakes: Two attempted Edits used identical old/new strings and were rejected without changes. RCA: drafted an edit before defining the intended replacement; fixed by writing the actual regression assertions in subsequent valid edits. An initial Python test ran `uv` from the repository root, where pytest is not installed; RCA: omitted the sidecar project directory; fixed by passing `uv run --directory` with the absolute sidecar path and frozen environment (5 tests passed). The first Vitest filter also matched snapshot copies under `.superpowers`, including one with an absent sibling import; RCA: Vitest matches filename substrings recursively, not only the named path; fixed by excluding `.superpowers` and confirming one named test file (6 tests). A timestamp test assumed successive writes differ at millisecond resolution; RCA: both writes could occur in one millisecond after the application-owned clock replaced caller backdating; fixed by separating the test writes and retaining separate persisted collection/narrative fields. No permission, hook, unrelated work, or production data change followed these mistakes.
- Task 2 reviewer identity recurrence: The ledger paired returned parallel agent IDs with the wrong roles, repeating the earlier order-association mistake. RCA: dispatch ordering was treated as identity rather than verifying returned task names/assignments. Fix: reconciled every completed notification and the TS agent's retained assignment; corrected ledger/report (spec a746ccd5f27e95f55, Rust a70456667db75164a, Python a5c106a876acfa2d4, TS ad62a5b3ac27780df, security a39b1ce03294deaba). No duplicate reviews. Some review anchors exceed actual source lengths; use current-source symbols, not diff-document positions as source-line citations.
- Task 2 fix-resume infrastructure failure: The original implementer could not resume because its API prompt exceeded context and automatic compaction returned an empty summary. RCA: exhausted worker transcript/context, not a source-code failure. Fix: verified all 12 pre-fix snapshot hashes unchanged and empty index, recorded an infrastructure-recovery ruling, and dispatched a fresh standard implementer at the same fix round with current brief/report/source grounding. Replacement completed fix1 and fix2 with focused passing evidence; no runtime/source-authentication acceptance inferred.
- Task 2 review-found contract/test defects: Mixed UTF-8-byte/code-point limits made Unicode content/focus disagree; StoredMessage role/feedback invariants were permissive and Rust admission put assistant-style run identity on operator rows; changed TS test spread an optional viewport into a required field. RCA: validators/admission/frontend fixture were not tested together under strict shared boundary cases, and build tsc excludes tests. Fix1 aligned code-point text/byte-safe focus, run-owned message binding and strict private changed-test checking; genuine 201-row/unknown-field fixtures and distinct-ID sequence conflicts replaced misleading coverage. Independent spec/Rust/Python/security fix1 reviews approved; TS confirmed these fixes after focused strict checker exit0. Root checker exit2/out-of-slice errors remain not proved baseline or green.
- Task 2 review-found trust defects: Generic apply accepted evidence provenance and terminal run-state payloads merely with an active lease/valid union. RCA: record-shape/lease checks were mistaken for authority to mint diagnostic source evidence or confirm process completion. Fix1 separated trusted application diagnostic/process-owner entry points from model proposals, without a model-writable trust flag; independent security/Rust/spec reviews approved the separation. Actual source authentication and gateway/worker/process wiring remain Tasks3/5/6; no live-worker exploit or runtime isolation acceptance claimed.
- Task 2 remaining parser defects: Required fields checked with `in` could be inherited, map skipped sparse-array holes, and stored-message validators allowed empty blocks/assistant media unlike actual write paths. RCA: shape validation assumed JSON-like own/dense containers and validated stored fields without reusing the relevant role/block invariants. Fix2 requires own enumerable fields/dense arrays in shared TS helpers and consistent nonempty/text-only-assistant blocks in all3 languages, preserving operator attachments. Intentional REDs failed on inherited/empty cases; reported final cards9/local5/Python5/ONE TS7 plus strict private/build typechecks pass. All five scoped fix2 reviews approved, including spec/quality; zero open findings in Task2's contract/persistence scope. No quantitative coverage or future-worker acceptance.
- Task 3 diagnostic-fixture assumptions: The initial synthetic Splunk search test used the XDR integration ID, and its positive request assumed JSON even though the existing Splunk client sends form data; an initial XDR POST whitelist entry also named production Cisco XDR despite its audited incident search being GET. RCA: copied a synthetic POST fixture across integration identities/formats before checking the current transports. Fix: bind search tests to Splunk and form encoding, remove the incorrect production XDR POST entry, and add a separate fixed Cisco Workflows diagnostic POST test; synthetic XDR POST remains labeled test-only. The initial gateway also read an entire response before applying its size cap; RCA: bounded retained content was mistaken for bounded network consumption. Fix: stream response chunks with a size cap and close responses, verified by a failing-then-passing 1 MB mocked response check. No live requests, unrelated edits, staging or permission changes.
- Task 3 fix-round-1 diagnostic boundary defects: Independent reviews showed the initial Requests timeout could be prolonged by trickled bytes, Requests prepared redirect responses after eagerly reading their bodies, and descriptor-only checks left short/keyed credentials, oversized nested results, POST query/auth templates and synthetic XDR doors insufficiently constrained. RCA: treated Requests inactivity timeout and `allow_redirects=False` as hard transport guarantees; verified post-response outcomes without checking actual bytes consumed; validated dynamic requests more strictly than discovery; copied synthetic XDR permissions into production policy. Fix: use a disposable spawned process per actual HTTP request with direct adapter dispatch, parent deadline/termination/reaping and pre-body redirect rejection; centralize short/Basic/Bearer/token/key/metadata redaction, bound characterized page/auth/request shapes and Splunk export NDJSON, share static discovery admission, and remove synthetic XDR doors. Synthetic loopback/adapter regressions verify actual blocked-I/O interruption and zero eager redirect consumption; real devices/providers, packaging and Tasks 4–6 remain unverified. Initial final stability loop returned pass@1/pass@2 63/63, then pass@3 failed because the 1.5-second synthetic socket budget expired before the spawned child reached the local server under scheduling load; RCA: test startup timing was conflated with the blocked-read measurement. Increased the synthetic read budget to five seconds and held the server response open for ten, retaining server-observed I/O and bounded cleanup assertions. Retained final evidence is three unchanged-source combined63-test runs, all exit0/no warnings; scoped independent re-review remains pending, not runtime/packaging acceptance.
- Task 3 fix-round-2 review-found diagnostic defects: Spawned HTTP children skipped the normal OS TLS trust bootstrap; an empty successful Splunk export was rejected; discovery advertised redacted, uncallable IDs/argument names; case-sensitive Basic/Bearer extraction leaked bare credentials and an empty derived value could corrupt visible fields; named-only response-array bounds missed Zabbix `result` and unrecognized envelopes. RCA: child initialization assumed parent SSL setup survived `spawn`, successful export was equated with at least one event, metadata redaction was not paired with shared availability checks, scheme parsing was exact-case, and record admission trusted an envelope-name allowlist. Fix: invoke the existing TLS helper inside the child before HTTP; admit only truly empty export as zero-row evidence while preserving strict nonempty NDJSON; fail closed in shared discovery/execution eligibility on dispatch-name collisions; normalize supported schemes, decode Basic components and discard empty derived variants; bound every nested array. Synthetic behavioral tests cover all five findings and both discover-to-execute and error paths. The first combined regression after the five focused fixes had 2 failures because two old tests deliberately used now-forbidden secret/dispatch-name collisions; corrected only their fixtures to retain their redaction assertions, without weakening fail-closed policy. Real TLS/devices/providers and Tasks 4–6 remain unverified.
- Task 3 fix-round-3 Basic credential redaction: Review found that valid Basic credentials encoded as Latin-1 by the installed Requests helper could expose non-ASCII plaintext in evidence. RCA: the shared secret extractor strictly decoded Base64 but interpreted the resulting bytes only as UTF-8, while the client supports Latin-1. Fix: retain UTF-8 extraction and also derive nonempty plaintext components using Latin-1; keep strict Base64, scheme handling, admission and transport unchanged. A test constructs synthetic Basic headers using Requests' helper and UTF-8, then checks response keys/values, errors, discovery and owned evidence slices. No live configuration, provider or device was accessed; review remains pending.
- Task 3 fix-round-1 bookkeeping scope: The worker incidentally shortened an existing controller mistake-log sentence while inserting adjacent Task3 notes. RCA: the replacement span included existing process prose beyond the necessary new entry. Fix: controller restored the original wording from the captured fix-before image; no product code changed for this restoration. Keep surgical process-log additions and preserve earlier issue/evidence wording.
- Task 3 controller review-bookkeeping recurrence: The first report creation was rejected before execution for missing explicit first-touch facts, and an initial HEAD-to-working-tree mistake-log diff included unrelated prior work and produced a large truncated output. RCA: relied on same-turn search/context instead of the required explicit four-fact presentation, and selected HEAD rather than the captured task-before log for review evidence. Fix: repeated the consumer/duplicate/absence search, supplied importer/API/schema facts plus the complete verbatim continuation instruction, then created the report standalone without changing hooks/permissions or delegating the denial. Generated a task-before-only process diff from task-3-base and retained unrelated work; the truncated broad diff is not used as evidence.

- Task 3 vault save-path rejection: The session-save tool rejected an automatically generated wiki path whose title included a sensitive-looking security term, even though the note contained only code-review findings and no secret values. RCA: the descriptive title produced a path rejected by the vault's safety filter. Fix/disposition: did not retry through another path or bypass the filter; retained current findings in the local execution ledger/report and explicitly reported the Obsidian save as unsuccessful. The earlier successful vault note does not contain this latest fix-round state.

- Task 3 scoped graph attribution assumption: The first metadata audit treated Python extractor basename source_file values as foreign, producing an unnecessarily large metadata preview. RCA: assumed the explicit extraction root guaranteed repository-relative source_file values for every parser. Fix: kept both raw and normalized extraction artifacts; mapped only the two unique allowed basenames after matching canonical explicit-input node identities, then merged without widening source inputs. Publication occurred only after source/artifact hash checks; no root scan or model call.

# Mistake: Task 4 controller report creation required clearer target-specific facts (2026-10-20)

- What the issue/mistake was: The first task-4-report.md Write was rejected before execution despite a four-point preamble. The target remained absent; reviewers received inline evidence and were told the report did not exist.
- RCA of the Issue: The hook requested explicit importer/caller, API, schema and verbatim-instruction facts for the absolute target; it did not identify which earlier statement was deficient, so the precise rejected fact is unknown. Prior summarized context was not sufficient evidence that the current presentation met the gate.
- How you fixed it: Rechecked target absence, Markdown references and duplicate-purpose content; restated the full absolute target, workflow consumers and their line anchors, executable API/schema/date-format non-applicability and the complete current instruction. A standalone Write then succeeded. No hooks, permissions or exemptions changed; no alternate-tool or delegated bypass. The report preserves reported results separately from acceptance. Independent reviews subsequently returned four medium issues, which remain open pending a scoped fix round.

- Controller review-status edit mistakes: One ledger Edit supplied identical old/new text and a subsequent attempt mismatched the existing sentence's capitalization; both were rejected without changes. RCA: dispatched a placeholder replacement and reconstructed the match instead of using the retained exact text. Fix: used the exact capitalized sentence from the successful earlier Edit and applied the intended spec-approved/Python-security-pending status. No product or review evidence changed; avoid placeholder edits and copy exact match strings.

- Task 4 scoped graph identifier validation: The first two-file graph build failed closed before publication because the validator compared canonical node IDs with literal relative-path prefixes. RCA: Graphify canonical IDs are normalized slugs; I reconstructed the format instead of inspecting the installed ID constructor. Fix: inspected only returned attribution metadata and the installed graphify.ids.make_id / file-stem implementation, then reused the unchanged raw extraction with exact canonical-ID checks and the same unique basename allowlist. No source-input widening or repeated extraction; the original published graph hashes were independently confirmed unchanged after failure. Publication outcome is recorded separately in the execution ledger.

# Mistake: Task 4 Batch A pagination and authentication fidelity (2026-10-20)

- What the issue/mistake was: Independent review found four medium defects: A1 rejected legitimate comma-separated/unquoted Meraki next relations and terminal prev-only pages; A2 ignored NetBox JSON next and reported incomplete lists without truncated; A3 sent untrimmed static tokens unlike the corresponding clients; A4 rejected Splunk/Prometheus Basic authentication with a nonempty username and empty password.
- RCA of the Issue: Initial fixtures used one quoted next link and generic response envelopes, and authentication checks conflated nonblank configuration text with field-specific normalization and password requirements. Exact ordinary-client preparation semantics were not fully represented by tests.
- How you fixed it: Added 50 synthetic regression cases before production changes (RED 31 failed/19 passed, exit 1). Select one unambiguous next relation before retaining existing strict destination/query/cursor validation; no next stops successfully. Mark the two fixed NetBox list reads partial when JSON next is nonnull, without following body URLs. Strip only source-confirmed static-token fields; preserve raw password text and Requests Basic preparation, Splunk token precedence and Prometheus Basic precedence/tenancy. Targeted GREEN: 50 passed, exit 0; combined/stability verification is reported separately, not inferred here. Existing transport/process/TLS/deadline/array/page/redaction controls and unavailable operations remain unchanged.
- Fix-round source-search mistake: Searched a guessed nonexistent mcp_servers/netbox_mcp.py path, producing rg exit 2. RCA: inferred a vendor file naming convention rather than checking inventory. Fix: located actual NetBox sources first and verified its results/count/next envelope from existing react_code.py source; no saved configuration, private database or external service was accessed.

# Mistake: Task 4 assumed a shared Python fixture path (2026-10-20)

- What the issue/mistake was: Tried to read sidecar/tests/conftest.py, which does not exist.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed a conventional pytest fixture location instead of checking the repository inventory first.
- How you fixed it: Located actual conftest paths with a bounded file search; kept the Canvas tests self-contained and did not create a shared fixture or access saved configuration.
- Additional command/edit mistakes: A bounded source search used shell head instead of limiting output in the dedicated/search tooling; stopped using it. An Edit accidentally supplied identical old/new text and was rejected without changes; replaced it with the intended test addition. A RED test command was followed by successful source searches in one shell command, so the shell returned 0 despite pytest reporting 5 failed/5 passed; retained pytest's failure evidence and use standalone test commands thereafter.
- Fixture mistakes: The first Batch A implementation check returned 4 failed/244 passed because two XDR assertions indexed a nonexistent Requests form keyword, the hostile matrix treated valid ThousandEyes ID 10001 as invalid, and it treated Grafana's declared literal query as an unknown argument. RCA: generic negative cases and format names were not aligned with the characterized field/Requests contract. Fixed assertions to inspect data, use an out-of-safe-range integer, and submit a hostile query rather than reject legitimate input.
- Boundary/auth defects caught before acceptance: A RED regression showed enabled MCP identities could activate empty-credential descriptors (4 cases), and another showed Prometheus selected Bearer instead of Requests' actual Basic precedence (1 case). Source audit also found ISE requires explicit JSON Accept; its new assertion failed before correction. RCA: configuration-presence merging was mistaken for executable binding, and ordinary session preparation/negotiation details were omitted. Fix: retain unavailable operation admission when app credentials are missing, preserve Basic precedence/tenancy, and add only the fixed JSON Accept door. No saved configuration, external endpoint, provider or device was called.

# Mistake: Task 4 remaining-A boundary and characterization omissions (2026-10-20)

- What the issue/mistake was: The first implementation rejected canonical MnT MAC paths because the shared path regex excludes colons. One synthetic token-mode Proxmox failure also asserted redaction of a password that was not configured for that integration. RCA of the Issue: Applied the generic path rule without its fixed MAC exception and reused a password fixture across authentication modes. How you fixed it: Permit canonical uppercase MACs only in the exact ISE MnT template, without widening the shared regex; assert the actual synthetic Proxmox token secret.
- What the issue/mistake was: Initially conflated ACI's nested login request with a required nested JSON login response. RCA of the Issue: Treated the synthetic response shape as protocol authority even though AciClient and StealthwatchClient authenticate from HTTP status/cookies, not JSON bodies. How you fixed it: Added RED cases for empty/non-JSON/unused nested bodies, kept cookie-only authentication authority, and observe bounded JSON token fields solely for scrubbing. Malformed nested request templates remain denied before transport.
- What the issue/mistake was: Cookie handling initially ignored RFC default paths and checked Expires even when Max-Age takes precedence; secondary Proxmox status responses also lacked the SDK data-object envelope check. RCA of the Issue: Implemented only the first happy-path cookie/status fixtures. How you fixed it: A focused RED run reported 6 failed/3 passed; derive default cookie paths from the fixed login route, honor Max-Age precedence, and mark malformed secondary status partial without fabricated defaults.
- What the issue/mistake was: API-issued response cookies/tokens were not initially included in whole-evidence scrubbing, and a cookie parser exception could expose its sensitive text before derived secrets were returned. RCA of the Issue: Secret observation covered login credentials only and relied on outer exception redaction before response-secret registration. How you fixed it: Added RED coverage (9 failures for issued credentials, then a separate injected parser-error failure), observe bounded sensitive response headers/cookie values without adopting them as authority, scrub secondary-issued credentials across the complete evidence, classify Cookie/CSRF/XSRF keys as sensitive, and sanitize parser errors. An initial parser fixture incorrectly assumed a colon-containing cookie name would throw; replaced that assumption with explicit stdlib parser fault injection.
- What the issue/mistake was: ACI reported totals and FMC paging-next metadata initially did not mark a retained slice partial. RCA of the Issue: Confused refusing secondary URLs with evidence completeness. How you fixed it: Added a 3-failure RED regression and conservatively mark those reported omissions partial; never follow the body URLs or infer request authority.
- Test-output mistake: Parameterized oversized XML content produced truncated failure output. Fixed parameter IDs to short labels and captured the initial RED summary with standalone --tb=no --no-summary (107 failed/281 deselected, exit 1); do not infer missing counts from truncated output. All configuration and transport fixtures remained synthetic; no ordinary client/getter, saved database, device or provider was called.

# Mistake: Task 4 remaining-A review fixes RA1–RA6 (2026-10-20)

- RA1 — Issue: Scrub observations were returned only after complete validation, so later malformed/oversized headers, rejected TFA/FMC authentication, or transport errors could leave retained partial evidence or refresh reasons unredacted. RCA: Credential acceptance and observation shared return-only state, and secondary failures were all treated as safely retainable. Fix: Keep collection-owned incremental scrub observations separate from credentials; observe auth JSON before acceptance; discard raw partial evidence and free-text refresh metadata on incomplete observation/transport failure. Preserve sanitized partial results only after complete header observation. Synthetic RED reproduced the rejected-auth and real direct_send pre-body boundaries; no rejected credentials, retries, redirects or TFA requests were adopted.
- RA2 — Issue: Positive Max-Age cookies could expire while login bodies were read and still be dispatched. RCA: Checked only nonpositive Max-Age, not elapsed lifetime. - RCA of the Issue: The gateway assumed the private executor always returned its bounded result envelope; its safety boundary began only after execute returned. Initial tests covered returned incomplete results but not raising executors or process/IPC cleanup seams.
- How you fixed it: Catch ordinary exceptions across native execution and observation admission at the trusted gateway boundary and convert them to the existing fixed _IncompleteObservation path without calling exception str/repr. Preserve established observations, drop refresh text, and retain/cache only fixed failed evidence. Added deterministic executor, start, early/late close and incomplete-result cases for gNMI/pyATS/Topolograph, including prior observations, cache and owned slices. Preserve other IPC close/join attempts with nested finally even when one close raises. No SDK, configuration or real startup failure probe was used.

# Mistake: Task4 amendment FIX1 R2 treated swallowed cleanup errors as an accepted limitation (2026-10-21)

- What the issue/mistake was: Real PyatsClient.disconnect_all swallowed is_connected/disconnect exceptions, allowing Canvas to record successful collection despite failed cleanup. The initial fake raising cleanup test did not exercise this real-wrapper behavior, and the previous report's limitation was not an accepted waiver of truthful cleanup reporting.
- RCA of the Issue: Assumed the existing wrapper exposed cleanup failure and tested a stronger fake contract instead of its actual implementation.
- How you fixed it: Add a keyword-only report_status flag on the existing wrapper. Default calls still swallow errors and return None; opted-in calls return one bounded boolean, attempt remaining devices and never collect or expose error text. Canvas explicitly requests status and requires True, otherwise retaining fixed Native cleanup failed evidence. Test the real wrapper with synthetic devices for is_connected/disconnect failures, connected/disconnected success, subsequent-device attempts, empty testbeds, invalid flag types, legacy swallowing and Canvas cache/slice behavior. No universal native non-mutation certification was added or reinstated.

# Mistake: Task4 amendment FIX1 R3 validated caller-owned arguments remained mutable (2026-10-21)

- What the issue/mistake was: Another thread could change an already-validated page or configured target before native execution, collecting different evidence under the old fingerprint and cache key.
- RCA of the Issue: Validation and fingerprinting used the same mutable caller dictionary later passed to the executor, without a private request snapshot.
- How you fixed it: Take one exact-dict shallow copy before argument validation, admit only exact string keys and string/integer scalar values, and use that same private snapshot through validation, fingerprinting, dispatch and spawn. Existing schema/type/range checks remain. Deterministic barrier tests mutate page1 to2 and configured target1 to2 and verify actual collected arguments, evidence, fingerprints, distinct cache entries and owned slices agree.

# Mistake: Task6 FIX1 prematurely marked feedback applied before delivery (2026-10-21)

- What the issue/mistake was: T6R5 committed applied state before sending the boundary reply. A synthetic worker exiting before accepting that reply left undelivered input applied rather than retained. Focused Rust RED1failed/15filtered demonstrated applied versus retained_for_continuation.
- RCA of the Issue: Equated selecting queued feedback in Rust with receipt by the worker's safe model boundary; a transport/cancel gap separated those events.
- How you fixed it: Boundary selection stays queued. After parsing/scoping the exact messages, the worker sends a receipt correlated to the boundary call UUID and ordered message IDs. Rust checks its pending batch and live revocation gate, atomically commits only those IDs, rejects replay/foreign receipts, and acknowledges before the worker returns its safe boundary to the next model request. Cancellation/lost delivery/invalid receipt/failed commit retain queued input. Later arrivals are not swept into an older receipt. Public schemas/runtime/tools/context are unchanged. Applied means worker receipt accepted at a safe boundary, not proof a provider consumed the next prompt; an already-received batch is not falsely relabeled undelivered merely because a later operation fails.

# Task8 corrective implementation mistakes (2026-10-22)

- What the issue/mistake was: Applied feedback could lose required priority after a receipt without model dispatch. Hydration checked optional image capacity after reading bytes and held run-control gates during I/O. Python counted ASCII escapes rather than UTF-8 wire bytes. Active image-free runs hid image-feedback exclusion, and successful close retained transient request accounting.
- RCA of the Issue: Receipt state was treated as sufficient to lower feedback priority; admission, storage reads and revocation were coupled. Framing serialization disagreed across languages, persisted run mode was absent from snapshots, and close did not reset all tab-scoped state.
- How you fixed it: Conservatively retain applied and retained feedback as required without claiming dispatch; native persisted-restart regressions cover budget rejection, cancellation and worker exit. Admit media budgets before reads, hydrate outside DB/manager/progress gates, and revalidate exact owner/generation/lease before handoff or receipt installation. Bounded read-counter/barrier regressions cover omission, metadata rejection and cancel/close/reopen. Match UTF-8 serialization; expose persisted image-free mode before feedback while retaining image/document selections; clear request accounting only on successful close. No migration or lifecycle redesign.

- What the issue/mistake was: The first lock-separation correction returned a storage-busy error during final admission revalidation without revoking its unstarted lease or reporting terminal persistence failure.
- RCA of the Issue: A newly fallible re-admission step used an early return outside the existing stop/retention path.
- How you fixed it: Added a failing finite DB-lock regression, then routed that failure through the unchanged stop_run owner. Revocation, sealed spawn authority, terminal-not-saved reporting and retained failure now remain truthful; restart reconciles the abandoned saved lease.

- What the issue/mistake was: New native fixtures imported adapters before consuming stdin, looked for a committed answer instead of the bounded stopped status, and initially cancelled the empty initial receipt. One source read requested a range beyond EOF.
- RCA of the Issue: Fixture startup and event assumptions did not match production's lazy adapter loading or the real host protocol.
- How you fixed it: Prefetch the bounded real pipe frame before adapter imports and pass it through actual CanvasWorker dispatch/validation; retain getter/network/SDK denial. Assert stopped status, wait for the post-model receipt before cancellation, and use the actual source range. These fixture/invocation failures are not behavioral RED evidence.

# Mistake: Telemetry Task 2 YAML boundary and local verification details

- What the issue/mistake was (2026-11-11): The initial conservative quote scanner misread a quote inside a valid plain scalar such as `title: foo{'junk` and could hide subsequent real YAML anchors/aliases. An early arithmetic evaluation vector needed an explicit f64 element type; the schema-check helper initially selected the worktree parent instead of the worktree root.
- RCA of the Issue: YAML node/quote boundaries were approximated without a token parser; inference could not resolve a vector used through unary negation; the fixture helper counted filesystem parents incorrectly. Initial contract generation also failed to split the digit-bearing sha256 field, leaving it private in Rust and using the boxed String type in TypeScript.
- How you fixed it: Retained a genuinely failing adversarial regression and replaced the handwritten scanner with the controller-authorized pinned yaml-rust2 token scanner before serde_yaml expansion. Reject real Anchor/Alias/Tag/TagDirective tokens and scanner errors, while allowing scalar literals/comments/block scalars. Kept bounded duplicate/depth/finite-value deserialization and added hostile nested arithmetic coverage. Annotated Vec<f64>, corrected the helper root and explicit public/string sha256 contracts, and reran scoped Rust/schema/production TypeScript checks. Original missing xterm dependency failure remains recorded; the controller restored only isolated worktree dependencies before the passing TypeScript recheck.

Reran focused definition/policy checks.

## 2026-11-11 — Telemetry Task 4 shared evaluation and verification

- What the issue/mistake was: Initial real-parser preview fixtures used uppercase IFACE/BYTES aliases even though the parser returns lowercase headers; five previews failed with missing_key. Self-review found finite scaled rates could become gaps because multiplication overflowed before time division, and explicit computes could not consume Unsigned base fields. Small initial Rust fixtures also hit a same-map borrow conflict and a moved String in a match guard; one exact edit anchor failed without changing a file. Two early controller read batches exceeded output budgets.
- RCA of the Issue: Fixture aliases did not follow the concrete parser output contract; arithmetic operation order and numeric-expression input handling were incomplete. Rust ownership rules and read/edit bounds were assumed rather than checked against the exact expressions and sections.
- How you fixed it: Corrected fixture aliases to iface/bytes and retained the actual parser-backed preview/live equality test. Added genuine failing arithmetic regressions, divided exact integer deltas by elapsed seconds before scaling, and admitted Unsigned values only into explicit numeric evaluation while retaining decimal counter fields and typed identity keys. Used sequential locals for the ownership fixes, retried the edit against actual context, and bounded subsequent reads to relevant sections/metadata without claiming truncated content was reviewed. Final focused results and open platform/runtime gates are recorded in task-4-report.md; no devices, credentials, running app or user configuration were accessed.

## 2026-11-11 — Telemetry Task 5 review fix 1: process privacy and CPU baseline alignment

- What the issue/mistake was: Pinned sysinfo's macOS process refresh unconditionally sysctl-reads PROCARGS2 argv/environment buffers before disabled retention flags; empty retained vectors did not prove no collection. An early Linux memory-only refresh advances process times while leaving its CPU denominator older, causing the next apparently valid percentage to underreport. A guessed Apple libproc source URL also returned404 without any local source mutation.
- RCA of the Issue: I checked the public refresh flags and retained vectors rather than the complete platform implementation and syscall boundary, and assumed a memory-only refresh could not mutate CPU baselines. The initial early regression checked only the immediate Missing value. The source URL was inferred instead of verified.
- How you fixed it: Retained real REDs: the owned test binary's sysctl interceptor counted568old-sampler PROCARGS2 requests while blocking payload return, and the early-refresh baseline assertion failed. With controller approval, added only a private native macOS process reader using verified SDK/libc proc_listpids/proc_pidinfo metadata/task APIs, never the sysinfo process refresher. Apple XNU confirms task CPU times and mach_absolute_time share Mach tick units; normalization uses their delta ratio, not guessed nanoseconds. Internal start-usec baselines reject reuse, public seconds contract retained. Clear process CPU eligibility after early refresh so the next eligible interval rewarms while memory remains refreshed; following interval becomes valid. Interceptor is test-only, has positive control/panic-safe reset, and never inspects payloads or panics across extern C. Corrected validation/report explicitly withdraw the initial privacy claim; final focused native tests/locked check and remaining Windows/Linux/lifecycle gates are recorded in the fix1 report. No other metrics/framework/dependencies or user app instrumentation were added.

## 2026-11-12 — Telemetry Task 6 review fix round1: complete log namespace and one trust snapshot

- What the issue/mistake was: The first logger policy preserved vendor Info/Warn and selected Debug records, following the earlier severity-preservation ruling. Actual pinned russh emits arbitrary unknown-request names at Info/Warn and pre-confirmation channel bytes at Debug. The host-trust marker check read the user path once, then the matcher reopened it, allowing an atomic replacement to mix validation with a different key set. A private Paramiko API inspection initially asked for a class attribute that is actually installed per instance.
- RCA of the Issue: Privacy was defined by a growing list of known log sites instead of the entire untrusted vendor namespace; the earlier preservation rule conflicted with the strict private/raw contract.
